- Rudolf Otto's formula, Shankara : Brahman : Ishvara : Atman :: Eckhart : Godhead : God : Soul, is Otto's (West-oestliche Mystik, 1926), not Coomaraswamy's. The claim that Coomaraswamy called Eckhart's sermons "the Upanishad of Europe" was removed as unconfirmed. [V as printed in a result, M, P03]
- Teresa's seven mansions: the title is given without a date in the essay's source list. If a date is added: written 1577, first printed 1588. [M, P18]

Sufi lines
- "He who knows himself knows his Lord" is cited as a hadith. Hadith scholars (Ibn Taymiyya, al-Nawawi and al-Suyuti, as I recall) say it is not established as a saying of the Prophet. It is cited by Ibn Arabi and the Sufis. Label it a saying. [M for the status, U for the attribution to Yahya ibn Mu'adh al-Razi, P06]
- "I was a hidden treasure and loved to be known" is not accepted as a hadith. Do not give it to the Prophet or quote it as Ibn Arabi's own teaching without the caveat. [M, P06 and P14]
- "You are not a drop in the ocean; you are the entire ocean in a drop" is widely given to Rumi. No source is known in the Mathnawi or the Divan. [U, P06]
- Rumi lines on quotation sites are mostly from loose English versions (Coleman Barks and others) and many have no Persian original. The "out beyond ideas of wrongdoing and rightdoing" line is of this kind. Use Nicholson or another named translation and give the Masnavi book and line. The four-men-and-a-coin story is genuine (Masnavi, Book II). [M and U, P13 and G1-4]
- Rumi's "mineral, plant, animal" passage is not a statement of Darwinian evolution. The Masnavi passage is III, around 3901 to 3906 in Nicholson's numbering; the idea also appears in a Divan poem and in many loose versions. [M, P16 and P18]
- "Wishing to unite, He separates to be able to unite; that is the function of Maya" circulates unattached and looks like the family of floating Rumi and Ibn Arabi lines. Not for use. [U, P02]
- The Simurgh pun is Attar's, not an etymology of the name. [M, P18]
- Ibn Arabi, Tarjuman al-Ashwaq, ode 11 ("my heart has become capable of every form") is a genuine line (Nicholson, 1911, public domain), but it speaks of love, not of a metaphysical One. Say which. [M for existence, U for details, P06]
- The phrase wahdat al-wujud ("the unity of being") is not found in Ibn Arabi's own writings as far as recalled. It was applied to his teaching by his school (Qunawi and later writers) and, as a charge, by his critics (Ibn Taymiyya). Chittick holds that the phrase is mostly not Ibn Arabi's own. [M, P04 and P15]

Other single lines
- Kabir lines in English are only partly traceable to the oldest manuscripts, and the 1915 Songs of Kabir comes from a Bengali collection. Write "attributed to Kabir". [M, P13]
- Ramakrishna's "yato mat tato path" is usually rendered "as many faiths, so many paths". The Gospel of Sri Ramakrishna is a disciple's diary in translation. Write "reported". [M, P13]
- Gandhi has many apocryphal one-liners online. Quote him only from Young India, Harijan, Hind Swaraj or the collected works, with a source. [M, P13]
- Teilhard de Chardin: "We are not human beings having a spiritual experience; we are spiritual beings having a human experience". No source is known in his writings and the origin is unsettled. Do not use it under his name. [U, G1-4]
- Einstein's 1950 letter is genuine as far as known but quoted in several wordings. Take the base text from a scholarly edition of his quotations. [M-, G1-4]
- Jung, "kindle a light in the darkness of mere being": the common paraphrase drops "mere". "Who looks outside, dreams; who looks inside, awakes" circulates widely; recalled as a 1916 letter to Fanny Bowditch [U]. "Individuation does not shut one out from the world, but gathers the world to oneself" is real, in CW 7, with the section number [U]. [M and U, P10]
- Campbell: "follow your bliss" is his (The Power of Myth). "The cave you enter holds the treasure you seek" circulates in several wordings and cannot be placed in his books. [U, P10]
- Engler: "you have to be somebody before you can be nobody" is a well-known line given to Engler. The book chapter is "Being Somebody and Being Nobody". The sentence was not seen in his text. [M, wording not cleared, P10]
- Katz is often shortened to "there are no pure experiences". The sentence has a parenthesis: "There are NO pure (i.e. unmediated) experiences." Katz does not deny that traditions use similar words. His claim is about the experience, not the vocabulary. [M, P11]
- Huineng's "originally there is not a single thing" comes from later editions of the Platform Sutra, not the Dunhuang text. [M, P18]
- Eliot's praise of Schuon: the gist is safe, the exact wording is not. [P04, P07]
- Two Guenon fragments circulate: one calling Buddhism the least interesting of the Eastern doctrines, and one saying karma never had the sense of causality. Both are cut from longer sentences and the book is uncertain (the Introduction or the Theosophy book). Do not quote them. The second overstates if read as denying moral consequence. The Buddhist counter-text is Anguttara Nikaya 6.63 (intention, cetana, is what is called kamma). [M, P02 and G1-3]
- Guenon's individualism sentence: quote only the clause after the lead-in and only from the printed book, because translations differ. The "limitless vista of possibilities" line attributed to the Introduction on a quote site is not safe. [U, P02]
- Sinnett as "genuinely fooled by all of Mme Blavatsky's tricks" is a fragment seen in a result. Do not quote. [C, P02]
- Huston Smith's "great prophet of our time" on Guenon is second hand through a review page. [U, P02]
- Coomaraswamy: "Verily" in the Shankara line may be a website's or translator's word. The 1916 "personality without a person" is lowered to [U] by G1-3. "Empirically speaking nothing transmigrates" is [V] with the source work uncertain (1916 or 1943). [V and U, P03]
- Schopenhauer's "solace" sentence and the Parerga section 184 passage: wording and locator are open. [P01]

### 14.3 Titles, dates and editions that get confused

- Huxley: The Perennial Philosophy (1945) is not Smith's Forgotten Truth, nor Schuon's Transcendent Unity of Religions, nor Perry's Treasury. The Doors of Perception (1954) and Heaven and Hell (1956) are two books, often cited as one. [M, P08 and G1-4]
- Smith's Forgotten Truth has two subtitles: 1976 "The Primordial Tradition" and 1992 "The Common Vision of the World's Religions" (the 1992 date is [U]). [M, P08, P09, G1-4]
- Perry's Treasury is by Whitall Perry. Smith wrote a Foreword to a Perry reissue, not the book. [M, P07]
- Schuon's The Transcendent Unity of Religions: French 1948, English 1953 (Faber), later edition with an introduction by Huston Smith. Whether Smith's piece is a Foreword or an Introduction, and in which edition, is open. It was reissued by a Theosophical press (Quest Books) [U]. A small irony, not a claim to use. [M and U, P04, P07, P09]
- "Time and Eternity" is the name of a chapter in Huxley's book and also of a separate 1947 book by Coomaraswamy (Artibus Asiae, Ascona). [M, P16]
- Underhill: Mysticism (1911), The Mystic Way (1913) and Practical Mysticism (1914) are three books. Part Two of the 1911 Mysticism is also titled "The Mystic Way". [M, G1-4 and P18]
- Zaehner's Mysticism Sacred and Profane (1957) is not Eliade's The Sacred and the Profane (German 1957, English 1959). Keep apart Stace's Mysticism and Philosophy (1960), Katz's Mysticism and Philosophical Analysis (1978) and Forman's The Problem of Pure Consciousness: Mysticism and Philosophy (1990). [M, P12]
- Watts, The Book on the Taboo Against Knowing Who You Are: the title has a colon. Eliade's Cosmos and History is not the 1954 English title. [M, P10]
- Campbell's monomyth has seventeen stages. The twelve-stage version is Vogler's. [M, P18]
- Stace's "introvertive" is not "Eastern" and "extrovertive" is not "Western". He finds both in both. [M, P11]
- Guenon's Man and His Becoming is 1925 in French. The origin of a 1927 date is unknown. The range "1932 to 1938" for the articles behind Perspectives on Initiation is [U]. [M and U, P02]
- Guenon: Le Theosophisme, histoire d'une pseudo-religion (1921) and L'erreur spirite (1923). Do not credit his attack on Theosophy and reincarnation to Perry, Smith or Coomaraswamy without a text. [M, P07 and G2-3]
- Coomaraswamy's Christian and Oriental Philosophy of Art is the 1956 Dover title of Why Exhibit Works of Art? Atmayajna is recalled as 1942 and Janua Coeli as 1939. [M, P03 and P18]
- The Mi'raj heavens come from hadith, not Qur'an 17:1, and the second heaven has two prophets. The Mithraic grades are Jerome's, not Origen's. [M, P18]
- Heart Sutra: the Sanskrit may be a back-translation from Chinese. [M, P18]

### 14.4 A tradition or a person said to teach what they do not

- Guenon and the Traditionalists reject reincarnation in the Western sense and wrote against the Theosophy that Alex also draws on. They are strong witnesses for the One, the veil and the return, and weak witnesses for "my soul comes back". Do not write "perennialists and Theosophists say" as one voice. Credit each with its own views. [M, P02, P05, P14, G2-3]
- Do not write that the Traditionalists dismiss Huxley. No sourced Traditionalist statement about Huxley is known. Write that the stricter school came first and asks more of a comparer. [U, P06]
- Huxley was not an initiate, not a Traditionalist and not a Theosophist, so Guenon's and Schuon's demand for initiation does not apply to him, and nothing they wrote against Theosophy applies to him. Do not credit Schuon's esoteric-unity thesis to Huxley, or Huxley's temperaments to Schuon. [M, P08, G1-4, G2-3]
- Do not claim Guenon's backing for Alex's approach. A writer who is not religious and quotes many traditions is what Guenon opposed. [P12]
- Coomaraswamy accepted a form of rebirth ("transmigration of character", not of a soul; wording from memory). His position on Western reincarnation is [U]. [P03 and P07]
- Do not credit Schuon for the fingers. That is Alex's reading of one-Self doctrine, better supported by Coomaraswamy's one-transmigrant paper. [M, P05]
- Do not write "Schuon's five steps". The count is the earlier researcher's reconstruction from several books. [U, P05]
- Guenon's Kali Yuga "more than six thousand years" is his own figure. The Puranic reckoning has 432,000 years from 3102 BCE. [M, P02]
- Hesiod's Works and Days has five ages (gold, silver, bronze, heroes, iron). The four-age sequence is Ovid's (Metamorphoses I). [M, P02]
- That the early Theosophical Society served British imperialism is Guenon's accusation, and the record is mixed (Besant founded the Home Rule League in 1916 and presided over the Indian National Congress in 1917). The Hodgson Report has itself been contested. [M, P02]
- Coomaraswamy's tie of matter and mother to the root of "measure" is his own etymology. Mainstream etymology derives Latin mater from the Proto-Indo-European word for mother. Attribute it to him. [M, P03]
- Coomaraswamy's Buddhism is contested by Theravada specialists. Walpola Rahula wrote that some try to smuggle the idea of atta into the Buddha's teaching and named Coomaraswamy and I. B. Horner. Exact words and page unknown. [V gist, P03]
- Huxley equates the Mahayana Dharmakaya or "Suchness" with the Ground, and Buddhist scholars would not accept that. Buddhist interdependence is not a "One". Smith keeps emptiness and Brahman apart in the chapters and joins them only in the levels, as recalled. [M, P08, P09, G1-4]
- Yoga is dualist, so kaivalya is not Advaita liberation. Dharma-megha as the tenth bhumi and Yoga Sutra IV.29 is a comparers' equation. [M, section 12.5]
- Mainstream Islam and Christianity teach one earthly life, then barzakh or purgatory, resurrection and judgement. Guenon and Schuon read those teachings as speaking of other states. The churches and the schools of law do not say so. Do not group them. [M, P16]
- Origen was not excommunicated for reincarnation. [verification pass, P01]
- Chabad does not say the world does not exist. Its teaching distinguishes a lower and a higher unity. Some scholars call the system acosmic (Rachel Elior, 1993 [U]). [M, P06]
- Tzimtzum is a sixteenth-century doctrine. Philippians 2 does not teach creation as self-limitation; later thinkers apply it. Adam ha-Rishon (all souls in the first man) is not the same figure as Adam Kadmon. [M, P06, P13, P14]
- The hadith of the believers as one body, Matthew 25:40 and 1 Corinthians 12:26: the Christian texts ground the unity in Christ, not in a shared Self. [M, P06]
- Campbell's monomyth "return" is a return to the world, not to the One. The rhizome image is not "separate plants, one root". Jung's Seven Sermons argue the opposite of the axiom's third clause. [M, P10]
- Wilber's Great Nest is not the Traditionalists' view, and the Traditionalists' descending view of history is not Wilber's. [M, P10]
- Schopenhauer's ethics ends in will-denial. Cialdini's oneness result is contested by Batson, who says no merging is needed. Levinas and Buber argue that a merged self has no one left to be responsible to. [M, P17]
- The Declaration toward a Global Ethic grounds the rule in interdependence, not oneness. [M, P17]
- Do not say James, Underhill and Muller are "cited by both camps". The experiential perennialists (Stace, Huxley) cite them; the contextualists (Katz, Proudfoot) criticise them. The Traditionalists rejected psychologising approaches like James's. [M, P12]
- Underhill's stages are her reading of the Christian mystics. Do not present them as "the stages of all mystics" and do not map them onto the arrow. [M, P11]
- "Seven is the number of rungs everywhere" is false. The counts range from four to a hundred. [M, P18]
- Other traditions draw staged maps too, so the scale is not unique to the school. That is safe. Do not extend it to "no other scale is centred on a door". [M, P18]
- Hindu inclusivism is older than Ramakrishna, but its modern universalist form is more recent. [M, P08]

### 14.5 Habits that prevent most of the above

- Copy every sentence from the book with edition and page. Do not copy it from a quotation site, a Goodreads page, a blog or this dossier. [P05, P02]
- Where a line comes through Huxley, check whether he was quoting someone else. [P13]
- Say "reports" and "texts", not "the same experience". Katz is the name a critic will cite. [P12]
- Use the tradition's own term, name the respect in which two things are compared, and report difference as well as match. [P12]

## 15. Open checks

This section is for Alex, or for a person with a normal internet connection or a library, before anything from this dossier is used in public. It repeats nothing from the findings beyond what is needed to say what to open.

### 15.1 What the dossier cannot settle

Nothing in the dossier was confirmed against a page or a book (section 1.1). Every date, title, locator, attribution and wording is a lead. The most reliable-looking items are the [V] and [C] ones, and those rest on search summaries seen once by a first-pass researcher.

"Best" cannot be settled by any file (5.7). The dossier supports Alex's view that Huxley is one anthologist among many by facts (not first, not strictest, not largest, no initiation, worked from translations). Whether Huxley is the best, or someone else is, stays Alex's judgement and should read as his opinion with reasons.

### 15.2 Decisions that are Alex's, not a fact-check

1. How to describe the movement: as one umbrella with strands that disagree (the dossier's reading), or as a short list of names. The first is safer (2.3, 12.3).
2. Whether to say in the essay that the Traditionalists would not endorse his method (they require one tradition, a lineage and initiation) and that they disliked the Theosophists. The files suggest saying it once (3.6, 14.4).
3. Which witnesses to use for lives. The Traditionalists are weak here. The files point to Coomaraswamy's one transmigrant for who carries over, and to the Indian, Platonic and Theosophical sources for repeated lives. The step from "something continues" to "many lives" is Alex's own, from his axiom (11.3).
4. Whether to add the "honest paragraph" on scholarly objection (Katz, Zaehner, Prothero, Hick, Griffiths and the rest, 13). The files suggest one sentence each.
5. Whether to include any [U] item. The dossier keeps a few because they help Alex's understanding. None should reach print until checked (15.5).
6. Whether to name Schuon, Guenon or Evola in print. Read Sedgwick first (13.4). The files could not check the controversies around Schuon's later community.
7. Which map of stages sits beside the arrow, and whether to say that other traditions draw maps too (safe) or that none centres on a door (not safe).
8. Authors who may be living (for example Wilber, Nasr, Shah-Kazemi, Ferrer, Kripal): say only what they have written themselves, in the spirit of the project's rule for living people.

### 15.3 First to check, in order

This list merges the "first to check" lists of the files. Open each at its source.

1. Every sentence that will sit in quotation marks. Start with the few [V] items in section 3.3: the Shankara dictum at the head of Coomaraswamy's 1944 paper ("no other transmigrant but the Lord"; check whether "Verily" is the translator's word), the 1916 passage in "Samsara and Kamma" (Heidelberg digital library pages 0158 to 0163), the Satan-essay sentence on denying ourselves, and the closing sentence of "Paths That Lead to the Same Summit" about approaching another believer as one already "of His".
2. Huxley's Foreword: the Leibniz error, the three-part definition, and the exact "Highest Common Factor" sentence.
3. The history of the name: Schmitt, "Perennial Philosophy: From Agostino Steuco to Leibniz", Journal of the History of Ideas 27 (1966); the Leibniz letter to Remond (1714 or 1715; the Rutherford translation); Steuco's Latin "one principle" sentence; whether the letter itself names Steuco.
4. Schuon's first chapters of The Transcendent Unity of Religions: the exoteric and esoteric thesis, the claim that the outer difference is on purpose, the triad of the Way, and the Eliot wording. Also whether Smith's piece is a Foreword or an Introduction.
5. Guenon on reincarnation and Theosophy: L'erreur spirite, Le Theosophisme, Man and His Becoming, The Multiple States of the Being. Pallis, Correspondence on Reincarnation (World Wisdom page; author, date and content unread). Lings on the hereafter: which book, if any, treats after-death states across traditions.
6. Coomaraswamy's paper "On the One and Only Transmigrant" (JAOS Supplement 3, 1944): whether it uses the thread-spirit (sutratman), and the Shankara line at its head. Also his Hinduism and Buddhism (1943), Time and Eternity (1947), and the exact year of Atmayajna.
7. Upanishad and scripture passages in a named translation (Olivelle for the Upanishads): Brihadaranyaka 1.4.1 to 4, 3.7.23 and 3.8.11; Chandogya 6.2 and 6.9 to 6.10; Isha 6 and 7; Mundaka 1.2.11, 3.1.1 and the knot verses; Katha; Taittiriya 2.7; Gita 2.22, 4.11, 6.29, 6.32, 7.21, 9.23, 13.28; Brahma Sutra 1.1.5, 2.1.32 and 2.1.33; Rigveda 1.164.46; Quran 5:48, 2:285, 2:156, 7:172, 89:27 to 30; Luke 15:17; Matthew 22:39, 25:40; Philippians 2; Tao Te Ching 16, 40, 48 (chapter numbers [U]).
8. The Secret Doctrine Proem (third fundamental proposition) with edition and page; Besant, The Four Great Religions; Blavatsky's own use of "blinds"; the Ego terminology trap; the Voice of the Silence wording.
9. Eckhart: sermon numbers and the "eye" saying (Sermon 12, [U]); Sermon 52 and the birth and the breakthrough (Walshe or McGinn); any line that came via Huxley (Quint's critical edition). The Angelus Silesius line should not be put under Eckhart.
10. Sufi texts: Burckhardt's Introduction to Sufi Doctrine (chapter titles, whether he makes Hindu equations); the earliest Sufi attribution and a clean English wording of the hidden-treasure saying; Chittick on Ibn Arabi's non-repetition and wahdat al-wujud; Rumi's grapes in Nicholson with the line number; the Masnavi III passage around 3901 to 3906; the Tarjuman ode 11.
11. Jewish sources: Schaya's French title and year, and whether he treats gilgul; Scholem and Idel on the Lurianic story (tzimtzum, breaking, sparks, tikkun, Adam ha-Rishon); the Chabad caution; Akiva against Ben Azzai (Sifra Kedoshim 4:12; Genesis Rabbah 24:7) on Sefaria.
12. Zhuangzi 7 in Watson or Graham; Yoga Sutras 1.12, 2.45, 4.3, 4.29 and 4.34; Plotinus 1.6.9; Marcus Aurelius 2.1, 8.34 and 11.8; Wang Yangming and Mencius for the fingers.
13. Emerson's Over-Soul sentence; Vivekananda's two Chicago sentences (vivekananda.net); Lessing's ring line and the Education of the Human Race sections 93 to 98; Schopenhauer's "solace" sentence and its locator (Parerga section 184, "True Christianity"); Numenius via Clement; Cusa's Latin formula; Yates's "shattered" sentence with its page.
14. Underhill's table of contents and where each of her stages sits; Teresa's Interior Castle (Seventh Mansions ch. 4); John of the Cross, Dark Night II.18 to 20; Dante, Paradiso I, XXI, XXVII, XXVIII and XXXIII in one edition; the Mi'raj hadith in Bukhari and Muslim; Tattvartha Sutra 10.5 to 8; AN 3.80; Bodhicaryavatara 3.25 and 10.55; the Heart Sutra Sanskrit in Conze with Nattier's article; Platform Sutra editions.
15. Scholarly sources: Katz's own sentence (and the page, 26) in Mysticism and Philosophical Analysis; Hick 1989 and Plantinga's reply; Forman 1990; Loy (do not state how many senses of non-duality he lists until the book is open); Sharf's three papers; Hanegraaff and Faivre on concordance; Sedgwick before naming Schuon, Guenon or Evola; Hacker on inclusivism; J. Z. Smith, Drudgery Divine (the "three parts" passage, as recalled around p. 51).
16. Wilber: Eye to Eye (injunction, apprehension, confirmation; the "Psychologia Perennis" essay; which edition holds what); Quantum Questions (whether the Schrodinger excerpts are from What Is Life?, Mind and Matter or My View of the World); the Great Nest and the level names.
17. Smith: the chapter titles of Forgotten Truth; the intermediate level and whether he names barzakh or bardo; whether the nested-circles diagram is his; the reissue year.
18. Indian systems: the Pratyabhijna-hrdayam sutra numbers (1 and 2 firm, 4 to 6, 9, 10, 17 to 20 best recall) in Singh or Wallis; the opening verse of the Isvara-pratyabhijna-karika; Ramanuja on the contraction and expansion of the soul's knowledge; Chaitanya-charitamrita Madhya 20.117; Vishnu Purana 6.7.61; the Yoga Vasistha stage list and locators.
19. Perry's Treasury: read the contents and index for its rebirth, karma and golden-rule headings, before any claim about what it contains.
20. Gita 13.28 and Shantideva 8.90 to 8.120 (numbering differs by edition): for the fingers and for the ethic of identity.

### 15.4 Quote and cite from the right kind of source

- Rights, from P11 lead 14: James (1902), Underhill (1911) and Bucke (1901) are public domain. Otto's 1932 translation may not be. Stace, Zaehner, Smart, Katz and Forman are in copyright, so quote briefly and check each sentence in print. Sacred Books of the East (Muller's series, which includes Jacobi's Jaina Sutras and the Upanishads, at sacred-texts.com) is public domain and Victorian in style. Nicholson's Tarjuman translation (1911) is public domain, and his Rumi is public domain in the UK (check later volumes in the US, [U]). Rights for any modern named translation (for example Olivelle, Walshe) are not covered by the files and need a separate check.
- Likely places the files name for primary texts, from memory and unchecked: Sefaria for Sifra, Shabbat 31a and Genesis Rabbah; sunnah.com for hadith; parliamentofreligions.org and charterforcompassion.org for the Declaration and the Charter; Heidelberg digital library for Coomaraswamy's 1916 book.
- For anything Rumi, use Nicholson or another named scholarly translation. For anything Eckhart, use Quint, Walshe or Colledge and McGinn. For Gandhi, use Young India, Harijan, Hind Swaraj or the collected works.

### 15.5 Items marked [U] that stay out of the essay until checked

- The Coomaraswamy post at the Museum of Fine Arts (the exact title and date); the 1916 "personality without a person" wording; his own position on Western reincarnation; what he wrote about Theosophy after leaving it.
- The Leibniz letter date (1714 or 1715).
- The claim that Pico's Oration contains "Plato is Moses in Attic Greek".
- Eliot's exact wording on Schuon; the Quest (Theosophical press) reissue of Schuon.
- Everything about Schuon's later community that is not in Sedgwick.
- Schopenhauer's Dresden or Weimar dates for meeting the Oupnek'hat.
- Smith's "great prophet of our time" on Guenon, and Smith's later subtitle year.
- Several Kabbalah and Sufi attributions: Lings pairing after-death states across traditions; Burckhardt making Hindu equations; Schaya on gilgul; Chabad and acosmism (Elior).
- The Jain "elephant" story used for many-sidedness (S5 flags the draft's sentence as resting on [U]). Also from S5, to add to the draft's elephant lines: in the Udana story the Buddha is the one who sees, and Rumi's elephant is about sense perception (P13).
- The Wittgenstein-based polythetic account: attributions to Alston, Saler and Needham.
- Hick's Death and Eternal Life proposing a series of lives in other worlds.
- Any count or list of Loy's senses of non-duality.
- The "Dara to Emerson" chain of transmission (not documented).

### 15.6 Claims not to make

- That Huxley is the best, or the first, or the coiner of the phrase.
- That "the Traditionalists dismiss Huxley".
- That the perennial philosophy movement as a whole teaches reincarnation, or that it is one voice with Theosophy.
- That any tradition "fits badly" with the axiom (Alex's correction in CLAUDE.md): show the inner layer, the close reading or the other vocabulary, and still say where a specialist objects.
- That all mystics have the same experience. Say that some ideas recur, as written, and that scholars still argue about the experience.
- That seven is the number of rungs everywhere.
- That a tradition says something it does not. Check the specialist objection for Buddhism, Jainism, Judaism, Christianity and Islam before each line.

### 15.7 A suggested order of work

1. Mark every sentence in the essay's perennial-philosophy paragraph and Part two that is in quotation marks, names a date, a title, a verse or a person.
2. Open each at its source (15.3) and write the edition and page beside it.
3. Replace any wording that cannot be found with a paraphrase.
4. Run the traps in section 14 over the final text, one by one.
5. Where two files disagreed (James Lecture XVI against XVII, Karika 3.5 against 3.8, Gita 2.13 against 2.22, Brahma Sutra 2.1.32 against 2.1.33, Schopenhauer's dates, the Huxley chapter numbers), settle it by the page and note which reading was right.
6. Tell Alex which of his own claims survived and which need softer wording.
